  - remaining disagreement: 3/6 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: evolution, logic, structure
  - raised by lenses: semantics
  - issue statement: The ontology lets Exposure.ltv drift from Exposure.amount and Collateral.appraised_value without saying whether calculated, entered, or overridden LTV is authoritative. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml: Exposure.ltv Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:24-37,39-46 Source finding context: `ltv` is named as a collateral ratio but modeled as an independent underwriter input while its source concepts coexist in the ontology. Source finding context: A ratio field stored as an independent input can diverge from the amount and appraisal values that define the risk meaning of the ratio. For a shared ontology, this makes it unclear whether LTV is authoritative input, derived value, or historical snapshot. Source finding context: Define `ltv` as a derived or snapshot attribute with explicit formula, denominator source, timestamp, and authority. If manual override is allowed, split it from calculated LTV, for example `calculated_ltv` and `approved_ltv_override`, with override authority and reason. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002 Source finding context: Shared semantic basis for risk-engine and reporting use of collateralized exposure risk measures. Source finding context: When Exposure.amount or Collateral.appraised_value changes, the independently entered `ltv` can retain a different value without the ontology saying which value is authoritative. Source finding context: Risk rules and reports can use inconsistent LTV semantics for the same exposure, reducing trust in classification and limit monitoring outputs. Source finding context: The ontology names `ltv` as a ratio-like risk measure but gives it input-field semantics instead of derived/snapshot semantics. Source finding context: Exposure contains `amount`, and Collateral contains `appraised_value` and `appraised_at`. Source finding context: Exposure also contains `ltv` with the note that it is entered by an underwriter at review time. Source finding context: Because the target does not define formula, timestamp, or override semantics for `ltv`, the ratio can drift from its source concepts.
  - affected purpose: Shared semantic basis for risk-engine and reporting use of collateralized exposure risk measures.
  - failure condition: Exposure.amount or Collateral.appraised_value changes while the independently entered ltv value remains different, or a consumer needs to decide whether to trust calculated or entered LTV. Source finding context: When Exposure.amount or Collateral.appraised_value changes, the independently entered `ltv` can retain a different value without the ontology saying which value is authoritative.
  - impact: Risk rules and reports can use inconsistent LTV semantics for the same exposure, reducing trust in classification and limit-monitoring outputs. Source finding context: Risk rules and reports can use inconsistent LTV semantics for the same exposure, reducing trust in classification and limit monitoring outputs.
  - root hypothesis: LTV is named as a collateralized ratio but modeled as an independent underwriter-entered input rather than a derived or snapshot measure with formula, timing, authority, and override semantics.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002`, `materialized-input.md:24-37`, `materialized-input.md:39-46`, `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:24-37,39-46`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-014`, `finding-014.cause-001`, `materialized-input.md:29`, `materialized-input.md:45-46`, `finding-014.cause-002`, `materialized-input.md:35-37`, `finding-014.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.axiology`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.evolution`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-009`, `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-004`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-009.structure`, `Direct structure evidence for the exact valuation/link construct if issue-009 is later treated as a standalone structural graph defect.`, `Formal logic evidence if issue-009 is later claimed as a pure contradiction rather than an authority/provenance issue.`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-004`, `rel-015`, `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: semantics
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: The declared purpose depends on a shared semantic basis for collateralized exposure risk measures. If Exposure.amount or Collateral.appraised_value changes while an independently entered ltv remains unchanged, risk engines and reports can apply different meanings of LTV to the same exposure, weakening classification, limit monitoring, and trust in outputs.
  - root cause: The starting point is that LTV has ratio semantics but is modeled as an independent underwriter-entered field, without formula, denominator source, valuation source, timestamp, authority, or override semantics. That modeling choice creates the ambiguity before any downstream consumer uses the value.
  - causal path: The ontology includes Exposure.amount and Collateral.appraised_value/appraised_at, which are plausible source concepts for an LTV ratio. It also includes Exposure.ltv as an entered review-time value. Because the target does not say how ltv relates to those source concepts, the entered value can drift from the calculated ratio and consumers cannot tell whether to trust the entered value, a derived value, or a historical snapshot.
  - action: Define LTV as a governed derived or snapshot measure with explicit formula, denominator and valuation source, timestamp, and authority. If manual override is allowed, split calculated LTV from the approved override and include override reason, approval authority, and related metadata. Preserve the dependency on the broader exposure-collateral valuation/link lifecycle, but do not let that broader dependency postpone fixing LTV authority semantics in the target model.
  - unresolved disagreement: Deliberation resolved the issue as standing independently, while preserving narrower concerns from evolution, logic, and structure: direct structure evidence would be needed to claim a standalone structural graph defect, and formal logic evidence would be needed to claim a pure contradiction.

- issue-010 (medium): The ontology materially fails to distinguish three non-equivalent credit grade concepts: borrower internal rating, engine-produced borrower rating, and exposure reporting grade. Because these are presented as loosely related credit/risk grades without canonical mapping or authority, consumers can treat incompatible scales as if they were interchangeable.

  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, semantics

  - issue statement: Three non-equivalent rating scales are all presented as credit/risk grades without a canonical semantic mapping or distinct concept names. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml: credit rating concepts Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:20-22,31-34,48-57,92-99 Source finding context: These are not merely alternate labels; they encode different authorities, units, and possibly different subjects: borrower-level internal rating, engine-produced borrower rating, and exposure-level reporting grade. Leaving them as loosely related grades makes same-looking 'rating/grade' semantics depend on the consuming system. Source finding context: Name the concepts by subject and authority, such as `borrower_internal_rating`, `engine_borrower_rating`, and `exposure_reporting_grade`; add an explicit canonical mapping/derivation artifact with owner, effective time, and override semantics for report-side adjustments. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-003 Source finding context: A concept authority shared by the risk engine and reporting system for credit-grade classification. Source finding context: A consumer must interpret or convert rating scales locally because the ontology does not establish one canonical semantic mapping or distinguish adjusted report grades from engine grades. Source finding context: Risk engine outputs and reporting classifications can disagree while both appear valid under the ontology, weakening the ontology as the shared concept standard. Source finding context: The ontology preserves historically separate rating scales without assigning a canonical semantic authority or explicit mapping inside the shared concept artifact. Source finding context: Borrower, RiskRating, and Exposure each define a grade/rating field with distinct scales and subjects. Source finding context: The classification rule says Exposure.risk_grade derives from RiskRating.grade but can be manually adjusted by the reporting team. Source finding context: The notes say the three scales coexist for historical reasons and conversion is performed by each consuming system.
  - affected purpose: A concept authority shared by the risk engine and reporting system for credit-grade classification.
  - failure condition: A consumer must interpret or convert rating scales locally because the ontology does not establish one canonical semantic mapping or distinguish adjusted report grades from engine grades.
  - impact: Risk engine outputs and reporting classifications can disagree while both appear valid under the ontology, weakening the ontology as the shared concept standard.
  - root hypothesis: The ontology preserves historically separate rating scales without assigning a canonical semantic authority or explicit mapping inside the shared concept artifact.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-003`, `materialized-input.md:20-22`, `materialized-input.md:31-34`, `materialized-input.md:48-57`, `materialized-input.md:92-99`, `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:20-22,31-34,48-57,92-99`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-015`, `finding-015.cause-001`, `finding-015.cause-002`, `materialized-input.md:92-93`, `finding-015.cause-003`, `materialized-input.md:97-99`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.axiology`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.evolution`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-010`, `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-001`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-010.structure`, `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: semantics
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: This weakens the ontology’s declared purpose as a shared concept authority for credit-grade classification. If the risk engine and reporting system both see their grade values as valid but no shared semantic mapping defines how they relate, each consumer must interpret or convert ratings locally, allowing inconsistent classifications to coexist under the same ontology.
  - root cause: The root cause is that the ontology preserves historically separate rating scales without assigning a canonical semantic authority or explicit mapping inside the shared artifact. That is the starting point because the downstream ambiguity follows from leaving separate historical scales unnamed, unmapped, and authority-ambiguous rather than modeling them as distinct concepts with governed derivation semantics.
  - causal path: The causal path starts with Borrower, RiskRating, and Exposure each defining grade or rating fields with different subjects and scales. Exposure.risk_grade is then described as derived from RiskRating.grade but also manually adjustable by reporting, which creates a report-side value that is not necessarily the same as the engine grade. The notes further state that the three scales coexist historically and that conversion is performed by each consuming system, confirming that the ontology delegates semantic reconciliation to consumers instead of owning it as the shared concept standard.
  - action: Fix the issue by naming the concepts according to subject and authority, for example borrower_internal_rating, engine_borrower_rating, and exposure_reporting_grade, and by adding a canonical mapping or derivation artifact. That artifact should define the mapping owner, effective time/versioning, and override semantics for reporting adjustments so engine output, borrower-level rating, and report-grade classification have explicit relationships rather than implicit consumer-specific conversions.

- issue-011 (medium): RiskAppetite compliance is materially under-modeled: RiskAppetite should represent the policy threshold, while compliance should be a separate scoped evaluation result over an ExposureAggregate at a specific time.

  - issue statement: The ontology cannot reliably compute, audit, or traverse RiskAppetite compliance because the threshold, evaluation result, subject aggregate, and time basis are not separated and structurally connected. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml: RiskAppetite entity Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:69-83,92-94 Source finding context: RiskAppetite conflates a policy threshold with a compliance result state. Source finding context: A threshold policy and a breach/compliance observation have different semantics and lifecycles. Placing the status on RiskAppetite makes the appetite object appear to change from compliant to breached, instead of producing a status for a specific aggregate evaluated against the appetite. Source finding context: Keep RiskAppetite as the policy/threshold concept and introduce a separate evaluation concept such as `RiskAppetiteCompliance` with references to RiskAppetite and ExposureAggregate plus `as_of` and `status`. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-004 Source finding context: Shared concept authority for limit/appetite compliance across engine and reporting outputs. Source finding context: A consumer treats `RiskAppetite.compliance_status` as intrinsic to the appetite threshold rather than as an evaluation result for a specific exposure aggregate and time. Source finding context: Reports or engine checks can lose the subject and time basis of a breach, making compliance status ambiguous and hard to reconcile. Source finding context: The entity combines the meaning of risk appetite policy with the meaning of compliance evaluation result. Source finding context: ExposureAggregate is a borrower-level total exposure with an `as_of` date. Source finding context: RiskAppetite is defined as a threshold and also carries `compliance_status`. Source finding context: The definition states breach status is determined when ExposureAggregate exceeds RiskAppetite, making status an evaluation result rather than an intrinsic property of the appetite threshold. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml entities.RiskAppetite / relations Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:78 and .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:85 Source finding context: RiskAppetite is structurally orphaned despite being defined as the compliance target for ExposureAggregate. Source finding context: The declared target is a shared concept authority for a risk engine and reporting system. With no structural connection, consumers cannot traverse from borrower exposure totals to the appetite threshold or compliance status using the ontology itself. The evidence supports this because RiskAppetite is declared at lines 78-83, while the complete relations list at lines 85-90 connects Borrower, Exposure, Collateral, RiskRating, Limit, and ExposureAggregate but omits RiskAppetite entirely. Source finding context: Add explicit structural relations and/or ref attributes that connect RiskAppetite to the entity it constrains, for example ExposureAggregate -> RiskAppetite with a kind such as tested_against or governed_by, plus the required scope keys such as borrower segment, product type, currency, and as_of if those determine which threshold applies. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-001 Source finding context: The ontology's stated purpose as a shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: When a consumer needs to compute, audit, or report RiskAppetite compliance from ExposureAggregate values, the target threshold/status concept is not reachable from the exposure aggregate graph. Source finding context: This weakens review trust because a central compliance concept is present but disconnected; different consumers may invent separate joins or scoping assumptions, producing inconsistent compliance outputs from the same ontology. Source finding context: RiskAppetite was added as an entity and rule target without adding the required graph edge or reference that binds it to ExposureAggregate or another scoping authority. Source finding context: RiskAppetite is declared as an entity with threshold and compliance_status attributes. Source finding context: Its definition depends on a comparison between ExposureAggregate and RiskAppetite. Source finding context: The ontology's relations section omits any relation involving RiskAppetite, leaving that comparison outside the navigable structure.
  - affected purpose: Shared concept authority for limit/appetite compliance across engine and reporting outputs. Source finding context: The ontology's stated purpose as a shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems.
  - failure condition: A consumer needs to compute, audit, or report compliance from ExposureAggregate values, or treats RiskAppetite.compliance_status as intrinsic to the threshold rather than an evaluation result for a specific subject and time. Source finding context: A consumer treats `RiskAppetite.compliance_status` as intrinsic to the appetite threshold rather than as an evaluation result for a specific exposure aggregate and time. Source finding context: When a consumer needs to compute, audit, or report RiskAppetite compliance from ExposureAggregate values, the target threshold/status concept is not reachable from the exposure aggregate graph.
  - impact: Compliance status becomes ambiguous and disconnected, so consumers may invent separate joins or scoping assumptions and produce inconsistent breach outputs. Source finding context: Reports or engine checks can lose the subject and time basis of a breach, making compliance status ambiguous and hard to reconcile. Source finding context: This weakens review trust because a central compliance concept is present but disconnected; different consumers may invent separate joins or scoping assumptions, producing inconsistent compliance outputs from the same ontology.
  - root hypothesis: RiskAppetite compliance is modeled as an intrinsic status on a policy threshold and omitted from the navigable relation graph instead of as a scoped evaluation result over an ExposureAggregate at a time.
  - evidence: `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-004`, `materialized-input.md:69-83`, `materialized-input.md:92-94`, `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:69-83,92-94`, `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-001`, `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:7`, `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:78`, `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:85`, `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:78 and .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:85`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-016`, `finding-ledger.yaml#finding-017`, `rel-010`, `finding-relation-graph.yaml#rel-010`, `finding-016.cause-001`, `materialized-input.md:69-76`, `finding-016.cause-002`, `materialized-input.md:78-83`, `finding-016.cause-003`, `materialized-input.md:78-79`, `finding-017.cause-001`, `finding-017.cause-002`, `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:79`, `finding-017.cause-003`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.axiology`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.coverage`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.evolution`, `issue-ledger.yaml#issue-011`, `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-002`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.logic`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.semantics`, `issue-stance-matrix.yaml#stances.issue-011.structure`, `issue-ledger.yaml#dep-003`, `rel-011`, `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/issue-ledger.yaml`, `.onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/problem-framing.yaml`
  - source lenses: semantics, structure
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: This weakens the ontology's declared purpose as a shared concept authority for limit/appetite compliance across engine and reporting outputs. Because compliance status is embedded on the threshold and RiskAppetite is omitted from the navigable aggregate graph, consumers cannot reliably compute, audit, traverse, or reconcile breach status from ExposureAggregate values without inventing their own joins, scope assumptions, and timing basis.
  - root cause: The starting point is that RiskAppetite compliance is modeled as an intrinsic status on a policy threshold and left outside the relation graph, rather than as an evaluation result binding threshold, subject aggregate, scope, and time. That modeling choice collapses two concepts with different lifecycles: the appetite policy and the observed compliance outcome.
  - causal path: The causal path is supported by the paired semantic and structural findings. ExposureAggregate is a borrower-level total exposure with an as_of date; RiskAppetite is defined as a threshold but also carries compliance_status; and the definition makes breach status depend on comparing ExposureAggregate against RiskAppetite. That makes status an evaluation result, not an intrinsic attribute of the appetite. Separately, the relations omit RiskAppetite, so the comparison is not reachable through the ontology's graph. Together, the ontology loses the subject and time basis of compliance and leaves central breach outputs ambiguous.
  - action: Keep RiskAppetite as the policy/threshold concept and add a RiskAppetiteCompliance evaluation concept or explicit relation that binds RiskAppetite, ExposureAggregate, applicable scope keys, as_of/evaluation time, and status. This should be fixed in the target before release because downstream engine and reporting consumers need one authoritative structure for deriving and auditing compliance rather than parallel consumer-specific interpretations.

#### Action Candidates
- issue-001: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: The deliberation resolution keeps this as a broad governed rating-event authority issue with high severity and no remaining disagreement. Because it affects the shared authoritative rating value for both engine and reporting, it must be closed in the target ontology before the artifact can serve its declared purpose. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml classification_rules / Exposure.risk_grade Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:6-8,31-34,48-56,92-93; .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-8 Source finding context: 보고서 팀의 수동 risk_grade 조정 권한이 공유 개념 권위 목적과 어긋난다. Source finding context: 공유 권위 문서라면 엔진 산출 등급과 보고 등급이 언제, 왜, 누구 권한으로 달라지는지 동일하게 해석되어야 한다. 현재 규칙은 보고서 팀의 국소적 필요를 권위 체계 위에 올려놓아 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 서로 다른 등급을 정당한 값으로 사용할 수 있게 한다. 이는 사용자가 요청한 '공유할 개념 기준'과 권위 관점 검토 목적을 직접 약화한다. Source finding context: 수동 조정을 원본 risk_grade 값에 덮어쓰기 가능한 규칙으로 두지 말고, 별도 Override/Adjustment 개념으로 분리해 owner, approval authority, reason, source_grade, adjusted_grade, effective_at/as_of, expiry, precedence, audit_id를 정의하라. 엔진과 보고 시스템 모두 동일한 우선순위 규칙으로 원본 등급과 조정 등급을 해석하게 해야 한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-001 Source finding context: 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 공유할 여신 리스크 개념 권위 기준 제공. Source finding context: 보고서 팀이 시장 상황을 이유로 Exposure.risk_grade를 수동 조정했지만 리스크 엔진은 RiskRating.grade 기반 원 등급을 쓰거나, 조정의 권위/시점/우선순위를 다르게 해석하는 경우. Source finding context: 동일 익스포저에 대해 엔진과 보고서가 서로 다른 리스크 등급을 권위 있는 값으로 간주할 수 있어 공유 기준의 신뢰성과 운영 의사결정의 일관성이 약화된다. Source finding context: 등급 조정이라는 별도 권위 행위를 원본 등급 개념 안에 무조건 허용하면서 조정 권위와 우선순위를 모델링하지 않았다. Source finding context: Exposure.risk_grade는 RiskRating.grade에서 도출되지만 보고서 팀이 시장 상황에 따라 수동 조정할 수 있다. Source finding context: RiskRating.grade는 리스크 엔진 산출 등급이고 Exposure.risk_grade는 보고서 팀이 사용하는 등급으로 서로 다른 소비자 권위가 걸려 있다. Source finding context: 대상 문서는 리스크 엔진과 보고서 시스템이 공유하는 개념 권위 문서를 지향한다. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / classification_rules[0] and notes[0] Source finding context: Embedded materialized input: classification_rules[0]; notes[0]; RiskRating.grade note Source finding context: 등급 변환과 보고용 수동 조정에 대한 감사 증거 개념이 누락되어 통제 행위의 행위자·시각·근거를 추적할 수 없다. Source finding context: 수동 조정은 통제가 필요한 행위이며, 리스크 엔진 산출값과 보고 시스템 표시값을 달라지게 할 수 있다. 공유 개념 기준 문서가 조정의 감사 증거 범주를 포함하지 않으면 보고 값이 엔진 값과 다른 이유를 사후에 설명하거나 승인된 조정과 임의 변경을 구분하기 어렵다. Source finding context: ManualRatingAdjustment 또는 RatingOverride 엔티티를 추가하고 original_grade, adjusted_grade, actor, approver, adjusted_at, reason_code, evidence_ref, approval_status, effective_period를 포함한다. 등급 매핑도 MappingVersion/MappingApproval로 모델링해 위키 참조가 아니라 온톨로지 내 권위 개념으로 편입한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-002 Source finding context: 리스크 엔진 산출과 보고 시스템 사용 값 사이의 통제 가능하고 감사 가능한 공유 기준 Source finding context: 보고서 팀이 Exposure.risk_grade를 수동 조정하거나 리스크팀 매핑 테이블 변경으로 소비 시스템별 등급 변환 결과가 달라지는 경우 Source finding context: 수동 조정의 행위자·시각·근거가 개념화되지 않아 보고 등급 변경의 정당성, 승인 여부, 원천 등급 대비 차이를 검증할 수 없고 운영 리스크와 감사 대응 결함이 발생한다. Source finding context: 통제 행위인 등급 override와 매핑 변경을 독립된 감사 가능한 사건 개념으로 포함하지 않은 범위 결손 Source finding context: 온톨로지는 보고서 팀의 시장 상황 기반 수동 등급 조정을 허용한다. Source finding context: 등급 변환은 각 소비 시스템이 수행하고 매핑 테이블은 대상 온톨로지 밖의 리스크팀 위키를 참조한다. Source finding context: 대상 엔티티에는 수동 조정 이벤트나 매핑 변경 승인·증거를 담는 개념이 없다. Source finding context: 그 결과 엔진 등급과 보고 등급이 달라지는 행위의 감사 추적 범위가 비어 있다. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / RiskRating, Borrower.internal_rating, Exposure.risk_grade Source finding context: Embedded materialized input: Borrower.attributes.internal_rating note; RiskRating.attributes.grade note; Exposure.attributes.risk_grade note; notes[0]; classification_rules[0] Source finding context: 병존하는 3개 신용등급 스케일에 대해 유효기간, 이력, 매핑 기준시점 개념이 누락되어 과거 시점의 등급 재구성이 불가능하다. Source finding context: 대상 문서는 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 공유하는 개념 권위 문서를 지향한다고 선언한다. 그러나 보고서 팀이 Exposure.risk_grade를 사용하고 수동 조정할 수 있으며, 3개 등급 스케일 변환을 각 소비 시스템이 수행한다고 되어 있어 등급 값은 시스템·시점 의존적이다. 이 상태에서 유효기간과 이력 개념이 없으면 특정 보고 기준일이나 과거 의사결정 시점에 어떤 등급과 매핑이 권위 있었는지 복원할 수 없어 엔진 산출과 보고 값의 공유 기준이 약해진다. Source finding context: 등급 관련 개념을 RatingScale, RatingAssignment, RatingMappingVersion 같은 별도 개념으로 분리하고, 각 등급 부여 및 매핑에 effective_from/effective_to, calculated_at 또는 assigned_at, source_system, superseded_by를 추가한다. Exposure.risk_grade의 수동 조정도 원등급과 조정등급을 구분해 기준시점별로 보존한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-001 Source finding context: 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 공유할 신용등급 개념 기준 및 과거 기준일 보고 재현성 Source finding context: 등급 스케일 또는 매핑이 변경되거나 보고서 팀이 시장 상황에 따라 Exposure.risk_grade를 수동 조정한 뒤 과거 기준일의 등급을 재현해야 하는 경우 Source finding context: 공유 권위 문서가 현재 값만 표현하므로 엔진 산출 등급, 차주 내부등급, 보고용 익스포저 등급 중 어느 값이 특정 시점의 권위 값인지 판별할 수 없고, 과거 보고·감사·리스크 재계산의 신뢰성이 손상된다. Source finding context: 등급을 시점 의존 assignment와 versioned mapping으로 모델링하지 않고 여러 엔티티의 현재 속성으로만 둔 개념 범위 결손 Source finding context: 대상 온톨로지는 세 개의 신용등급 스케일을 병존시키고 각 소비 시스템이 변환한다고 설명한다. Source finding context: Exposure.risk_grade는 RiskRating.grade에서 도출되지만 보고서 팀의 수동 조정도 허용한다. Source finding context: 등급 속성에는 적용 기간, 산출/부여 시각, 매핑 버전, 변경 이력 개념이 없다. Source finding context: 따라서 특정 기준일의 권위 등급과 매핑을 재구성하는 데 필요한 시간성 범위가 온톨로지에 포함되지 않는다.

- issue-005: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: All applicable lenses accept the missing owned RatingScale/RatingMapping root, while severity disagreement is preserved only as lens-specific attribution. The final issue retains high severity due to evolution and replay impact, so target closure is required. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / classification_rules[0] and notes[0] Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `classification_rules` states `Exposure.risk_grade는 RiskRating.grade에서 도출하되, 보고서 팀이 시장 상황에 따라 수동 조정할 수 있다.`; `notes` states `차주 등급(internal_rating 1~10, RiskRating R1~R5, Exposure risk_grade AAA~CCC)의 3개 스케일은 역사적 이유로 병존한다. 변환은 각 소비 시스템이 수행한다.` Source finding context: Three rating scales are left to consumer-side conversion, so rating-scale evolution requires coordinated changes in every consuming system and can break continuity between engine and reports. Source finding context: Because the ontology is intended as the shared concept authority for a risk engine and reporting system, leaving scale conversion outside the ontology means any new grade, rating-model change, or report override policy change must be reimplemented across consumers. Historical results also cannot be consistently reinterpreted because there is no versioned conversion authority tying `RiskRating.grade`, `Borrower.internal_rating`, and `Exposure.risk_grade` together over time. Source finding context: Add a canonical `RatingScale`/`RatingMapping` concept with source scale, target scale, mapping rules, owner, version, effective_from/effective_to, and override provenance. Make `Exposure.risk_grade` derivation reference that mapping rather than consumer-local conversion. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-001 Source finding context: The ontology's declared purpose as a shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: When a rating scale changes, a new grade is introduced, or report-side manual adjustment policy changes, consumers must independently update conversion logic and may produce inconsistent or non-replayable classifications. Source finding context: This weakens review trust in the ontology as an extensible shared basis: the central classification concept is not change-tolerant, and engine/report continuity depends on undocumented external implementations. Source finding context: The ontology does not make rating conversion a versioned, owned concept inside the target artifact and instead delegates it to external consumer systems. Source finding context: The target contains three rating scales for borrower/internal, engine, and exposure/report classifications. Source finding context: The target does not define the conversion as an ontology entity or versioned relation; it points to an external wiki and each consumer system. Source finding context: Consumer-side conversion enables divergent behavior when grades or mapping rules evolve.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-008: fix_now
  - rationale: All participating lenses accept the root, and the final resolution retains high severity because subtype semantics can propagate incorrect exposure treatment to collateral assets. This is a current correctness blocker for the ontology's core type model. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / Collateral entity inheritance Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `Collateral.definition` says `익스포저를 담보하는 자산. 담보 평가의 기본 단위.` while `Collateral` also declares `is_a: Exposure`; relations include `{ from: Exposure, to: Collateral, kind: secured_by }`. Source finding context: Collateral is modeled both as a subtype of Exposure and as a secured asset related from Exposure, limiting future collateral model evolution. Source finding context: From an evolution perspective, this conflates credit exposure and securing asset lifecycles. Adding collateral-specific extensions such as pooled collateral, multiple valuations, guarantees as collateral support, lien priority, or asset substitution may require untangling inheritance because collateral would inherit exposure attributes like borrower reference, amount, product type, risk grade, and LTV even when those are not properties of the asset itself. Source finding context: Remove `Collateral is_a Exposure` and model collateral as a separate asset/support concept related to exposures through a relationship entity such as `SecurityAgreement` or `CollateralLink`, carrying allocation, priority, valuation, and effective dates. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-004 Source finding context: A change-tolerant concept basis for exposure and collateral management shared across systems. Source finding context: When collateral coverage expands beyond one asset directly attached to one exposure, the inherited exposure structure forces collateral records to carry exposure fields or requires a breaking remodel. Source finding context: The current inheritance choice narrows future collateral extensions and increases the likelihood of a disruptive schema change in a core risk-management concept. Source finding context: The ontology uses inheritance for an association/lifecycle relationship between exposure and collateral. Source finding context: Collateral is defined as an asset securing an exposure. Source finding context: The ontology simultaneously declares `Collateral is_a Exposure` and a separate `Exposure secured_by Collateral` relation. Source finding context: Inheritance makes collateral structurally depend on exposure attributes, constraining later collateral-specific lifecycle extensions. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / entities.Collateral Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `Exposure` definition; `Collateral` definition with `is_a: Exposure`; relation `Exposure -> Collateral` with `kind: secured_by`. Source finding context: `Collateral` is simultaneously modeled as an `Exposure` subtype and as the asset secured by an `Exposure`, creating a type-level conflict for the same entity role. Source finding context: The conflict is formed by explicit claims in the artifact: `Exposure` is the credit exposure, `Collateral` is the securing asset, `Collateral is_a Exposure`, and the relation `Exposure secured_by Collateral` uses Collateral as the secured asset target. These cannot all be satisfied without collapsing exposure and collateral asset into the same class, which weakens the ontology as a shared concept authority for a risk engine and reporting system because systems may inherit exposure attributes onto collateral records or count collateral as exposure. Source finding context: Remove `is_a: Exposure` from `Collateral` and keep collateral connected through the `Exposure -> Collateral secured_by` relation; if a shared parent is needed, introduce a neutral parent such as `RiskObject` only with attributes valid for both concepts. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-001 Source finding context: Shared credit-risk concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: When consumers interpret `is_a` as inheritance/subtyping, collateral assets become valid Exposure instances while also serving as assets securing exposures. Source finding context: This can make type-based consumers apply exposure fields, aggregation, or reporting logic to collateral records, undermining the declared shared taxonomy contract. Source finding context: The ontology uses `is_a` to connect Collateral to Exposure even though Collateral's own definition and relation role describe a secured asset, not a credit exposure. Source finding context: Collateral is declared as an asset that secures an exposure and as `is_a: Exposure`. Source finding context: Exposure is separately defined as the credit exposure to a borrower, and the relation declares Exposure as secured by Collateral. Source finding context: Together these claims require Collateral to be both a kind of Exposure and the asset target that secures an Exposure. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml: Collateral entity Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:39-46,85-88 Source finding context: Collateral is modeled as a subtype of Exposure even though its definition makes it a securing asset, not a credit exposure. Source finding context: A collateral asset and a credit exposure are different ontological kinds: one is the pledged asset, the other is the credit position being secured. Treating collateral as an Exposure weakens the shared concept standard because a risk engine or report can classify collateral records as exposure records or apply exposure attributes/aggregation semantics to assets. Source finding context: Remove `Collateral.is_a: Exposure`; keep Collateral as a separate asset entity connected through `Exposure secured_by Collateral`. If a shared superclass is needed, introduce a neutral parent such as `RiskObject` only after defining common semantics explicitly. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-001 Source finding context: A shared concept authority for risk engines and reporting systems covering exposure and collateral concepts. Source finding context: Any consumer that interprets `is_a` semantically will treat collateral assets as credit exposures despite the target also defining them as securing assets. Source finding context: This can corrupt classification, aggregation, and reporting semantics at the boundary between exposure records and collateral records. Source finding context: The subtype assertion uses `Exposure` as the parent for `Collateral` although the target definition assigns Collateral the meaning of a securing asset. Source finding context: Collateral is defined as an asset securing an exposure and the base unit of collateral appraisal. Source finding context: The same entity is declared `is_a: Exposure`. Source finding context: The relation section separately models Exposure as `secured_by` Collateral, confirming the intended distinction between exposure and collateral asset.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-002: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: The issue is materially supported without a deliberation conflict. It directly weakens cross-system consistency of grade meaning and conversion criteria, so the target should close it as part of the rating authority model rather than defer it to consumers. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml rating scale notes and RiskRating.grade Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:20-22,53-56,97-98; .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-8 Source finding context: 등급 스케일 변환을 각 소비 시스템에 맡겨 공유 개념 기준 목적을 훼손한다. Source finding context: 이 온톨로지의 목적은 엔진과 보고 시스템이 공유할 개념 기준을 제공하는 것이다. 그런데 등급 변환의 의미와 책임을 각 소비 시스템으로 분산하면 같은 차주/익스포저 등급이 시스템별 변환표, 버전, 적용일에 따라 달라질 수 있다. 이는 역사적 병존이라는 국소 현실을 보존하는 대신 공유 기준이라는 상위 목적을 양보한 숨은 트레이드오프다. Source finding context: 세 등급 스케일을 인정하되, canonical RatingScale과 RatingMapping 개념을 온톨로지 안에 추가하라. source_scale, target_scale, source_grade, target_grade, mapping_version, owner, effective_from/effective_to, precedence를 정의하고 외부 위키는 비권위 참고가 아니라 해당 규칙의 출처/운영 절차로만 연결하라. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-002 Source finding context: 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템 사이의 등급 의미 및 변환 기준 공유. Source finding context: 엔진, 보고서, 기타 소비 시스템이 각자 다른 변환표나 다른 시점의 위키 규칙으로 internal_rating/RiskRating/Exposure risk_grade를 변환하는 경우. Source finding context: 공유 온톨로지가 등급 의미의 단일 기준이 되지 못하고, 시스템별 해석 차이를 정당화하는 문서가 되어 보고 및 리스크 판단 간 불일치를 만든다. Source finding context: 등급 스케일 병존을 canonical mapping concept으로 승격하지 않고 소비 시스템별 변환 책임으로 남겼다. Source finding context: 차주 등급, 엔진 등급, 익스포저 등급의 세 스케일이 병존한다. Source finding context: 변환은 각 소비 시스템이 수행한다고 명시되어 있다. Source finding context: 온톨로지는 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 공유하는 개념 권위 문서를 지향한다.

- issue-003: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Axiology, coverage, and evolution support the missing shared time and FX authority root, with semantics narrowing only to directly evidenced time-scope semantics. The issue affects reproducible compliance/utilization signals, so it should be fixed in the target concept model. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml Limit utilization and ExposureAggregate temporality Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:64-76,78-83,92-95; .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-8 Source finding context: 한도 소진율과 RiskAppetite 준수 판단이 시스템별 환율표와 월말 배치 집계에 의존해 시간성·권위 목적에서 drift 된다. Source finding context: RiskAppetite 준수 여부는 운영상 민감한 개념인데, 입력 총액은 월말 기준으로 낡을 수 있고 환산 기준은 시스템별로 달라질 수 있다. 공유 개념 권위 문서의 목적상 같은 시점의 같은 포트폴리오에 대해 엔진과 보고 시스템이 같은 준수 상태를 재현해야 하지만, 현재 설계는 시간 기준과 환율 권위를 분산시켜 서로 다른 소진율과 위반 여부를 허용한다. Source finding context: Limit 소진율과 RiskAppetite 준수 판단에 canonical valuation_time/as_of, aggregation_cutoff, freshness_status, fx_rate_source, fx_rate_timestamp, currency_basis를 추가하라. 월말 배치 값은 stale 가능성을 명시하고, 일중 판단이 필요한 준수 상태에는 별도 current exposure source 또는 'not current' 상태를 모델링하라. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-003 Source finding context: 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 한도 소진율 및 RiskAppetite 준수 상태를 동일한 권위와 시간 기준으로 공유하는 것. Source finding context: 일중 신규 여신이 존재하거나 통화가 다른 한도/익스포저를 다룰 때, 시스템별 환율표와 월말 집계 시점 차이로 소진율 또는 breached/compliant 상태가 달라지는 경우. Source finding context: 위반 상태라는 핵심 운영 신호가 공유 온톨로지에서 재현 가능한 권위 값이 아니게 되어 보고 지연, 한도 초과 미탐지, 엔진-보고 불일치 위험을 만든다. Source finding context: 한도/준수 판단에 필요한 시간 기준과 환율 권위를 공유 개념으로 닫지 않고 소비 시스템과 배치 주기에 맡겼다. Source finding context: ExposureAggregate.total_amount는 월말 배치 저장 값이며 일중 신규 여신은 다음 배치까지 미반영된다. Source finding context: 통화가 다르면 각 시스템의 당일 환율 테이블로 Limit 소진율을 계산한다. Source finding context: RiskAppetite 준수 상태는 ExposureAggregate가 threshold를 초과하는지로 정의된다. Source finding context: 대상은 리스크 엔진과 보고서 시스템이 공유하는 개념 권위 문서를 지향하며, 검토 목적은 시간성·권위의 운영 위험을 포함한다.

The narrowed gap is still a material completeness issue for shared classification and reporting consistency. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / Exposure and notes[1] Source finding context: Embedded materialized input: Exposure entity; notes[1] Source finding context: 연체·부실 상태를 범위 밖으로 제외해 여신 리스크 분류의 상태 lifecycle이 정상 익스포저 중심으로만 닫히고, 부실 전이 이후의 리스크 상태 범주가 누락된다. Source finding context: 대상은 여신 리스크 관리의 개념 모델로서 익스포저, 등급, 한도, 담보를 정의하고 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 공유할 기준을 지향한다. 그러나 여신 리스크에서 익스포저 lifecycle의 핵심 상태인 연체·부실을 제외하면, 리스크 엔진 등급과 보고 시스템의 상태 기반 집계가 같은 차주/익스포저를 어느 단계로 분류해야 하는지 공유할 수 없다. 별도 시스템이 소유하더라도 최소한 외부 권위 상태 참조, 기준시점, 수신 이벤트 개념은 필요하다. - issue-006: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: The final resolution accepts this as a utilization calculation provenance and authority gap, primarily supported by evolution and coverage with axiology materiality support. Logic, semantics, and structure limits are evidence-scope caveats, not blockers to classifying the issue as a target-level root cause. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / ExposureAggregate and classification_rules[1] Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `ExposureAggregate.total_amount` note says `월말 배치로 계산해 저장. 일중 신규 여신은 다음 배치까지 미반영.`; classification rule says `Limit 소진율 = ExposureAggregate.total_amount / Limit.approved_amount. 통화가 다른 경우 환산은 각 시스템의 당일 환율 테이블을 사용한다.` Source finding context: Exposure aggregation and limit-utilization rules are not temporally versioned, so adding intraday products, currencies, or reporting cutoffs can break continuity of limit calculations. Source finding context: A credit risk taxonomy that must serve both engines and reports needs stable temporal semantics for replay, audit, and future product/currency expansion. Here, intraday exposures are explicitly excluded until the next batch, while limit utilization uses that stale aggregate and system-local FX. When new intraday facilities, currencies, or reporting frequencies are added, the existing concepts cannot absorb them without changing calculation semantics or accepting inconsistent utilization outputs. Source finding context: Introduce explicit temporal and valuation concepts: `ExposureSnapshot` or `ExposureAggregate` with calculation_frequency, included_exposure_cutoff, as_of timestamp, source batch/run id, and FX source/rate_time. Define limit utilization against a named snapshot and canonical FX policy. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-002 Source finding context: Shared risk-engine and reporting basis for limit utilization and exposure aggregation. Source finding context: When the ontology must support intraday lending, additional currencies, or different report cutoffs, the same borrower and limit can receive different utilization results depending on batch timing and each system's FX table. Source finding context: The ontology is not evolution-resilient for temporal expansion: future reporting or engine changes require reworking core aggregate semantics and may disrupt comparability with historical stored totals. Source finding context: The ontology combines stored batch aggregates with system-local FX conversion but lacks canonical snapshot/version and valuation-time concepts. Source finding context: `ExposureAggregate.total_amount` is defined as the total of all exposure amounts but is stored from a month-end batch that omits intraday new credit until the next batch. Source finding context: Limit utilization is computed from that aggregate and approved limit amount. Source finding context: Currency conversion is delegated to each system's same-day FX table, leaving no stable valuation authority in the ontology. Source finding context: Future intraday, multi-currency, or higher-frequency reporting changes can alter utilization results without a versioned snapshot or FX policy preserving continuity. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / Limit.approved_amount and classification_rules[1] Source finding context: Embedded materialized input: Limit.attributes.approved_amount note; classification_rules[1] Source finding context: 한도와 환율처럼 복수 시스템에 걸쳐 관리되는 값에 단일 원본 권위와 기준시점 개념이 누락되어 한도 소진율 계산 기준이 분산된다. Source finding context: Limit 소진율은 ExposureAggregate.total_amount / Limit.approved_amount로 정의되어 리스크 위반 판단과 보고에 직접 쓰일 수 있는 지표다. 그런데 구성 값의 단일 권위와 환산 기준이 없으면 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 같은 차주·같은 날짜에 서로 다른 소진율을 계산할 수 있으며, 공유 개념 기준으로서의 역할이 약해진다. Source finding context: LimitSourceAuthority 또는 BookOfRecord 개념을 추가해 승인 시스템, CRM, 환율 테이블의 역할과 우선순위를 명시한다. CurrencyConversionRate에는 rate_source, rate_timestamp, effective_date, currency_pair, version을 두고, LimitUtilization 계산에는 사용한 원천값과 환율 기준을 참조하게 한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-003 Source finding context: 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 공유하는 한도 소진율 및 통화 환산 기준 Source finding context: 승인 시스템과 CRM의 한도 값이 다르거나 각 시스템의 당일 환율 테이블이 다른 값을 제공하는 경우 Source finding context: 소진율 계산의 입력 권위와 환산 기준이 분산되어 동일 익스포저에 대해 시스템별 결과가 달라질 수 있고, RiskAppetite 위반 판단 또는 보고 숫자의 재현성이 떨어진다. Source finding context: 복수 시스템 관리 값과 환산 기준에 대한 권위 원본 및 우선순위 개념이 없는 범위 결손 Source finding context: Limit.approved_amount는 승인 시스템 값이지만 CRM의 available_limit과 합산 관리된다고 설명된다. Source finding context: 따라서 Limit 소진율을 공유 계산 기준으로 삼는 데 필요한 권위 범위가 닫혀 있지 않다.

- issue-007: follow_up
  - rationale: The issue is materially supported as evolution and coverage concern, but it is less immediately blocking than authority/replay defects. It should be carried forward as a managed-taxonomy improvement that may be closed during the next modeling stage. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / enumerated category attributes Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `Borrower.segment` values `[retail, sme, corporate]`; `Exposure.product_type` values `[term_loan, revolving, guarantee]`; `Collateral.collateral_type` values `[real_estate, deposit, securities]`; `Exposure.risk_grade` and `RiskRating.grade` are also closed enum lists. Source finding context: Core business classifications are modeled as closed inline enums, so new borrower segments, products, collateral classes, or grades require schema edits rather than additive extension. Source finding context: For an evolving credit-risk concept standard, categories such as product type, collateral type, segment, and grade are expected to expand as portfolios, regulations, and reporting needs change. Inline closed enums force changes directly into existing attribute definitions, increasing migration pressure and making it harder to preserve historical category meaning or support consumer compatibility. Source finding context: Promote volatile classifications to managed taxonomy concepts such as `ProductType`, `CollateralType`, `BorrowerSegment`, and `GradeCode` with stable ids, labels, active/deprecated status, version/effective dates, and parent/category relations. Keep attributes as references to those taxonomy entries. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-003 Source finding context: Extensible shared classification ontology for credit-risk engine and reporting concepts. Source finding context: When a new segment, product, collateral class, or grade is introduced, the ontology must edit existing enum declarations and downstream validators instead of adding a new taxonomy record with compatibility metadata. Source finding context: This creates brittle evolution and migration risk in the core classification surface, especially for systems that rely on the ontology as a shared validation and reporting basis. Source finding context: The ontology encodes volatile classification vocabularies as closed attribute-local enums instead of first-class extensible taxonomy concepts. Source finding context: Multiple core classification axes are closed inline enum lists inside attributes. Source finding context: Inline enum expansion requires modifying existing attribute schemas rather than adding separate taxonomy records. Source finding context: Without lifecycle or effective-version metadata, future category changes cannot preserve historical meaning or compatibility in the ontology itself.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-009: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: The issue stands independently while preserving its dependency on the broader exposure-collateral valuation/link lifecycle. It weakens authority and reproducibility for a shared risk measure, so it should be closed in the target model. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml: Exposure.ltv Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:24-37,39-46 Source finding context: `ltv` is named as a collateral ratio but modeled as an independent underwriter input while its source concepts coexist in the ontology. Source finding context: A ratio field stored as an independent input can diverge from the amount and appraisal values that define the risk meaning of the ratio. For a shared ontology, this makes it unclear whether LTV is authoritative input, derived value, or historical snapshot. Source finding context: Define `ltv` as a derived or snapshot attribute with explicit formula, denominator source, timestamp, and authority. If manual override is allowed, split it from calculated LTV, for example `calculated_ltv` and `approved_ltv_override`, with override authority and reason. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002 Source finding context: Shared semantic basis for risk-engine and reporting use of collateralized exposure risk measures. Source finding context: When Exposure.amount or Collateral.appraised_value changes, the independently entered `ltv` can retain a different value without the ontology saying which value is authoritative. Source finding context: Risk rules and reports can use inconsistent LTV semantics for the same exposure, reducing trust in classification and limit monitoring outputs. Source finding context: The ontology names `ltv` as a ratio-like risk measure but gives it input-field semantics instead of derived/snapshot semantics. Source finding context: Exposure contains `amount`, and Collateral contains `appraised_value` and `appraised_at`. Source finding context: Exposure also contains `ltv` with the note that it is entered by an underwriter at review time. Source finding context: Because the target does not define formula, timestamp, or override semantics for `ltv`, the ratio can drift from its source concepts.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-010: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Applicable lenses support the semantic rating-scale authority problem, and the issue overlaps with but is not eliminated by the broader rating conversion issue. It should be fixed in the target because grade concept naming and mapping are central to shared interpretation. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml: credit rating concepts Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:20-22,31-34,48-57,92-99 Source finding context: Three non-equivalent rating scales are all presented as credit/risk grades without a canonical semantic mapping or distinct concept names. Source finding context: These are not merely alternate labels; they encode different authorities, units, and possibly different subjects: borrower-level internal rating, engine-produced borrower rating, and exposure-level reporting grade. Leaving them as loosely related grades makes same-looking 'rating/grade' semantics depend on the consuming system. Source finding context: Name the concepts by subject and authority, such as `borrower_internal_rating`, `engine_borrower_rating`, and `exposure_reporting_grade`; add an explicit canonical mapping/derivation artifact with owner, effective time, and override semantics for report-side adjustments. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-003 Source finding context: A concept authority shared by the risk engine and reporting system for credit-grade classification. Source finding context: A consumer must interpret or convert rating scales locally because the ontology does not establish one canonical semantic mapping or distinguish adjusted report grades from engine grades. Source finding context: Risk engine outputs and reporting classifications can disagree while both appear valid under the ontology, weakening the ontology as the shared concept standard. Source finding context: The ontology preserves historically separate rating scales without assigning a canonical semantic authority or explicit mapping inside the shared concept artifact. Source finding context: Borrower, RiskRating, and Exposure each define a grade/rating field with distinct scales and subjects. Source finding context: The classification rule says Exposure.risk_grade derives from RiskRating.grade but can be manually adjusted by the reporting team. Source finding context: The notes say the three scales coexist for historical reasons and conversion is performed by each consuming system.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`
- issue-011: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: All participating lenses converge on the evaluation-result framing, with logic's earlier narrowing absorbed into the intended aggregate-derived compliance scope. The missing semantic and structural binding can produce inconsistent breach outputs, so target-level closure is required. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml: RiskAppetite entity Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:69-83,92-94 Source finding context: RiskAppetite conflates a policy threshold with a compliance result state. Source finding context: A threshold policy and a breach/compliance observation have different semantics and lifecycles. Placing the status on RiskAppetite makes the appetite object appear to change from compliant to breached, instead of producing a status for a specific aggregate evaluated against the appetite. Source finding context: Keep RiskAppetite as the policy/threshold concept and introduce a separate evaluation concept such as `RiskAppetiteCompliance` with references to RiskAppetite and ExposureAggregate plus `as_of` and `status`. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-004 Source finding context: Shared concept authority for limit/appetite compliance across engine and reporting outputs. Source finding context: A consumer treats `RiskAppetite.compliance_status` as intrinsic to the appetite threshold rather than as an evaluation result for a specific exposure aggregate and time. Source finding context: Reports or engine checks can lose the subject and time basis of a breach, making compliance status ambiguous and hard to reconcile. Source finding context: The entity combines the meaning of risk appetite policy with the meaning of compliance evaluation result. Source finding context: ExposureAggregate is a borrower-level total exposure with an `as_of` date. Source finding context: RiskAppetite is defined as a threshold and also carries `compliance_status`. Source finding context: The definition states breach status is determined when ExposureAggregate exceeds RiskAppetite, making status an evaluation result rather than an intrinsic property of the appetite threshold. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml entities.RiskAppetite / relations Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:78 and .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:85 Source finding context: RiskAppetite is structurally orphaned despite being defined as the compliance target for ExposureAggregate. Source finding context: The declared target is a shared concept authority for a risk engine and reporting system. With no structural connection, consumers cannot traverse from borrower exposure totals to the appetite threshold or compliance status using the ontology itself. The evidence supports this because RiskAppetite is declared at lines 78-83, while the complete relations list at lines 85-90 connects Borrower, Exposure, Collateral, RiskRating, Limit, and ExposureAggregate but omits RiskAppetite entirely. Source finding context: Add explicit structural relations and/or ref attributes that connect RiskAppetite to the entity it constrains, for example ExposureAggregate -> RiskAppetite with a kind such as tested_against or governed_by, plus the required scope keys such as borrower segment, product type, currency, and as_of if those determine which threshold applies. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-001 Source finding context: The ontology's stated purpose as a shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: When a consumer needs to compute, audit, or report RiskAppetite compliance from ExposureAggregate values, the target threshold/status concept is not reachable from the exposure aggregate graph. Source finding context: This weakens review trust because a central compliance concept is present but disconnected; different consumers may invent separate joins or scoping assumptions, producing inconsistent compliance outputs from the same ontology. Source finding context: RiskAppetite was added as an entity and rule target without adding the required graph edge or reference that binds it to ExposureAggregate or another scoping authority. Source finding context: RiskAppetite is declared as an entity with threshold and compliance_status attributes. Source finding context: Its definition depends on a comparison between ExposureAggregate and RiskAppetite. Source finding context: The ontology's relations section omits any relation involving RiskAppetite, leaving that comparison outside the navigable structure.
  - derivation refs: `issue-ledger.yaml`, `problem-framing.yaml`

### Consensus (6/6, core-axis mode)
- issue-001 (high): Issue-001 is a material governed rating-event authority gap: the ontology cannot provide one shared authoritative rating value when engine grades, report-side manual adjustments, mapping changes, and historical assignments are modeled as mutable/current grade attributes instead of distinct governed events.
  - issue stance agreement: 4/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: coverage, evolution, semantics, axiology
  - issue stance disagreement: 0/6
  - disagreeing stance lenses: none
  - not applicable lenses: structure
  - insufficient evidence lenses: logic
  - resolution accepted by: 5/5 deliberation participants
  - accepted lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, semantics, logic
  - remaining disagreement: 0/5 deliberation participants
  - remaining disagreement lenses: none
  - raised by lenses: axiology, coverage
  - issue statement: The ontology cannot establish a shared authoritative rating value when report-side manual adjustments, historical rating assignments, and mapping changes are not represented as distinct governed events. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml classification_rules / Exposure.risk_grade Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:6-8,31-34,48-56,92-93; .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-8 Source finding context: 보고서 팀의 수동 risk_grade 조정 권한이 공유 개념 권위 목적과 어긋난다. Source finding context: 공유 권위 문서라면 엔진 산출 등급과 보고 등급이 언제, 왜, 누구 권한으로 달라지는지 동일하게 해석되어야 한다. 현재 규칙은 보고서 팀의 국소적 필요를 권위 체계 위에 올려놓아 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 서로 다른 등급을 정당한 값으로 사용할 수 있게 한다. 이는 사용자가 요청한 '공유할 개념 기준'과 권위 관점 검토 목적을 직접 약화한다. Source finding context: 수동 조정을 원본 risk_grade 값에 덮어쓰기 가능한 규칙으로 두지 말고, 별도 Override/Adjustment 개념으로 분리해 owner, approval authority, reason, source_grade, adjusted_grade, effective_at/as_of, expiry, precedence, audit_id를 정의하라. 엔진과 보고 시스템 모두 동일한 우선순위 규칙으로 원본 등급과 조정 등급을 해석하게 해야 한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-001 Source finding context: 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 공유할 여신 리스크 개념 권위 기준 제공. Source finding context: 보고서 팀이 시장 상황을 이유로 Exposure.risk_grade를 수동 조정했지만 리스크 엔진은 RiskRating.grade 기반 원 등급을 쓰거나, 조정의 권위/시점/우선순위를 다르게 해석하는 경우. Source finding context: 동일 익스포저에 대해 엔진과 보고서가 서로 다른 리스크 등급을 권위 있는 값으로 간주할 수 있어 공유 기준의 신뢰성과 운영 의사결정의 일관성이 약화된다. Source finding context: 등급 조정이라는 별도 권위 행위를 원본 등급 개념 안에 무조건 허용하면서 조정 권위와 우선순위를 모델링하지 않았다. Source finding context: Exposure.risk_grade는 RiskRating.grade에서 도출되지만 보고서 팀이 시장 상황에 따라 수동 조정할 수 있다. Source finding context: RiskRating.grade는 리스크 엔진 산출 등급이고 Exposure.risk_grade는 보고서 팀이 사용하는 등급으로 서로 다른 소비자 권위가 걸려 있다. Source finding context: 대상 문서는 리스크 엔진과 보고서 시스템이 공유하는 개념 권위 문서를 지향한다. Source finding context: 수동 조정은 통제가 필요한 행위이며, 리스크 엔진 산출값과 보고 시스템 표시값을 달라지게 할 수 있다. 공유 개념 기준 문서가 조정의 감사 증거 범주를 포함하지 않으면 보고 값이 엔진 값과 다른 이유를 사후에 설명하거나 승인된 조정과 임의 변경을 구분하기 어렵다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-002 Source finding context: 리스크 엔진 산출과 보고 시스템 사용 값 사이의 통제 가능하고 감사 가능한 공유 기준 Source finding context: 보고서 팀이 Exposure.risk_grade를 수동 조정하거나 리스크팀 매핑 테이블 변경으로 소비 시스템별 등급 변환 결과가 달라지는 경우 Source finding context: 수동 조정의 행위자·시각·근거가 개념화되지 않아 보고 등급 변경의 정당성, 승인 여부, 원천 등급 대비 차이를 검증할 수 없고 운영 리스크와 감사 대응 결함이 발생한다. Source finding context: 그 결과 엔진 등급과 보고 등급이 달라지는 행위의 감사 추적 범위가 비어 있다. Source finding context: 대상 문서는 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 공유하는 개념 권위 문서를 지향한다고 선언한다. 그러나 보고서 팀이 Exposure.risk_grade를 사용하고 수동 조정할 수 있으며, 3개 등급 스케일 변환을 각 소비 시스템이 수행한다고 되어 있어 등급 값은 시스템·시점 의존적이다. 이 상태에서 유효기간과 이력 개념이 없으면 특정 보고 기준일이나 과거 의사결정 시점에 어떤 등급과 매핑이 권위 있었는지 복원할 수 없어 엔진 산출과 보고 값의 공유 기준이 약해진다.   - source lenses: axiology, coverage
  - action candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - materiality: This weakens the declared purpose because the artifact is meant to give the risk engine and reporting system a shared, reproducible credit-rating concept authority. If Exposure.risk_grade can be manually adjusted for reporting, mappings can change outside the ontology, and past ratings lack effective-period and audit metadata, then engine output, reporting display, and historical reconstruction can each look valid while pointing to different authoritative values.
  - root cause: The root cause is the under-modeling of rating changes, mappings, assignments, and report-side overrides as current grade attributes. That is the starting point because the missing event concepts are where authority, owner, approval, precedence, effective period, source grade, adjusted grade, timestamp, and audit evidence would need to live for both systems to interpret the same rating value consistently.
  - causal path: The causal path starts with Exposure.risk_grade being derived from RiskRating.grade while also allowing report-team manual adjustment. The ontology also keeps multiple rating scales and leaves conversion to consumer systems, with mapping references outside the target artifact. Because rating attributes do not carry effective periods, assignment history, mapping versions, or override evidence, the model cannot determine which grade was authoritative at a given time, who changed it, why it changed, or whether the change was approved. That supports the issue that engine grades, reporting grades, and replayed historical grades can diverge without a common authority trail.
  - action: Fix this before the target ontology is considered complete by separating RatingAssignment, RatingMappingVersion or MappingApproval, and ManualRatingAdjustment or RatingOverride from current grade attributes. These concepts need owner/source authority, original and adjusted grade, approval authority/status, reason, effective period, timestamp, precedence, source system, supersession or versioning, and audit evidence so engine and reporting consumers use the same priority and replay rules.

- issue-005 (high): Issue-005 is a material shared-authority failure: the ontology keeps three rating scales but leaves conversion among them to consumer systems, so rating-scale changes can split engine and reporting behavior and make historical classifications non-replayable.
  - issue stance agreement: 4/6
  - agreed or narrowed lenses: axiology, coverage, evolution, semantics
