Source finding context: 따라서 특정 기준일의 권위 등급과 매핑을 재구성하는 데 필요한 시간성 범위가 온톨로지에 포함되지 않는다.
  - failure condition: A report team manually adjusts Exposure.risk_grade, a rating mapping changes, or a past reporting date must be reconstructed after grade or override activity occurred. Source finding context: 보고서 팀이 시장 상황을 이유로 Exposure.risk_grade를 수동 조정했지만 리스크 엔진은 RiskRating.grade 기반 원 등급을 쓰거나, 조정의 권위/시점/우선순위를 다르게 해석하는 경우. Source finding context: 보고서 팀이 Exposure.risk_grade를 수동 조정하거나 리스크팀 매핑 테이블 변경으로 소비 시스템별 등급 변환 결과가 달라지는 경우 Source finding context: 등급 스케일 또는 매핑이 변경되거나 보고서 팀이 시장 상황에 따라 Exposure.risk_grade를 수동 조정한 뒤 과거 기준일의 등급을 재현해야 하는 경우
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Fix this before the target ontology is considered complete by separating RatingAssignment, RatingMappingVersion or MappingApproval, and ManualRatingAdjustment or RatingOverride from current grade attributes. These concepts need owner/source authority, original and adjusted grade, approval authority/status, reason, effective period, timestamp, precedence, source system, supersession or versioning, and audit evidence so engine and reporting consumers use the same priority and replay rules.
  - remediation: Fix this before the target ontology is considered complete by separating RatingAssignment, RatingMappingVersion or MappingApproval, and ManualRatingAdjustment or RatingOverride from current grade attributes. These concepts need owner/source authority, original and adjusted grade, approval authority/status, reason, effective period, timestamp, precedence, source system, supersession or versioning, and audit evidence so engine and reporting consumers use the same priority and replay rules.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
- issue-005 (high)
  - issue: Issue-005 is a material shared-authority failure: the ontology keeps three rating scales but leaves conversion among them to consumer systems, so rating-scale changes can split engine and reporting behavior and make historical classifications non-replayable.
  - target: Three rating scales are left to consumer-side conversion, so rating-scale evolution requires coordinated changes in every consuming system and can break continuity between engine and reports. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / classification_rules[0] and notes[0] Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `classification_rules` states `Exposure.risk_grade는 RiskRating.grade에서 도출하되, 보고서 팀이 시장 상황에 따라 수동 조정할 수 있다.`; `notes` states `차주 등급(internal_rating 1~10, RiskRating R1~R5, Exposure risk_grade AAA~CCC)의 3개 스케일은 역사적 이유로 병존한다. 변환은 각 소비 시스템이 수행한다.` Source finding context: Because the ontology is intended as the shared concept authority for a risk engine and reporting system, leaving scale conversion outside the ontology means any new grade, rating-model change, or report override policy change must be reimplemented across consumers. Historical results also cannot be consistently reinterpreted because there is no versioned conversion authority tying `RiskRating.grade`, `Borrower.internal_rating`, and `Exposure.risk_grade` together over time. Source finding context: Add a canonical `RatingScale`/`RatingMapping` concept with source scale, target scale, mapping rules, owner, version, effective_from/effective_to, and override provenance. Make `Exposure.risk_grade` derivation reference that mapping rather than consumer-local conversion. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-001 Source finding context: The ontology's declared purpose as a shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: When a rating scale changes, a new grade is introduced, or report-side manual adjustment policy changes, consumers must independently update conversion logic and may produce inconsistent or non-replayable classifications. Source finding context: This weakens review trust in the ontology as an extensible shared basis: the central classification concept is not change-tolerant, and engine/report continuity depends on undocumented external implementations. Source finding context: The ontology does not make rating conversion a versioned, owned concept inside the target artifact and instead delegates it to external consumer systems. Source finding context: The target contains three rating scales for borrower/internal, engine, and exposure/report classifications. Source finding context: The target does not define the conversion as an ontology entity or versioned relation; it points to an external wiki and each consumer system. Source finding context: Consumer-side conversion enables divergent behavior when grades or mapping rules evolve.
  - failure condition: When a rating scale changes, a new grade is introduced, or report-side manual adjustment policy changes, consumers must independently update conversion logic and may produce inconsistent or non-replayable classifications.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Add a canonical `RatingScale`/`RatingMapping` concept before release, including source scale, target scale, mapping rules, owner, version, effective_from/effective_to, and override provenance. `Exposure.risk_grade` derivation should reference that mapping rather than consumer-local conversion, so engine/report continuity and historical replay depend on a single versioned ontology authority.
  - remediation: Add a canonical `RatingScale`/`RatingMapping` concept before release, including source scale, target scale, mapping rules, owner, version, effective_from/effective_to, and override provenance. `Exposure.risk_grade` derivation should reference that mapping rather than consumer-local conversion, so engine/report continuity and historical replay depend on a single versioned ontology authority.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
- issue-008 (high)
  - issue: Collateral is materially mis-modeled because the ontology makes it both a subtype of Exposure and the asset that secures an Exposure. This collapses two different core concepts: the credit position being secured and the collateral asset/support that secures it.
  - target: Collateral is assigned incompatible roles as both an Exposure subtype and a securing asset related from Exposure, creating a type-level and lifecycle modeling conflict. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / Collateral entity inheritance Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `Collateral.definition` says `익스포저를 담보하는 자산. 담보 평가의 기본 단위.` while `Collateral` also declares `is_a: Exposure`; relations include `{ from: Exposure, to: Collateral, kind: secured_by }`. Source finding context: Collateral is modeled both as a subtype of Exposure and as a secured asset related from Exposure, limiting future collateral model evolution. Source finding context: From an evolution perspective, this conflates credit exposure and securing asset lifecycles. Adding collateral-specific extensions such as pooled collateral, multiple valuations, guarantees as collateral support, lien priority, or asset substitution may require untangling inheritance because collateral would inherit exposure attributes like borrower reference, amount, product type, risk grade, and LTV even when those are not properties of the asset itself. Source finding context: Remove `Collateral is_a Exposure` and model collateral as a separate asset/support concept related to exposures through a relationship entity such as `SecurityAgreement` or `CollateralLink`, carrying allocation, priority, valuation, and effective dates. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-004 Source finding context: A change-tolerant concept basis for exposure and collateral management shared across systems. Source finding context: When collateral coverage expands beyond one asset directly attached to one exposure, the inherited exposure structure forces collateral records to carry exposure fields or requires a breaking remodel. Source finding context: The current inheritance choice narrows future collateral extensions and increases the likelihood of a disruptive schema change in a core risk-management concept. Source finding context: The ontology uses inheritance for an association/lifecycle relationship between exposure and collateral. Source finding context: Collateral is defined as an asset securing an exposure. Source finding context: The ontology simultaneously declares `Collateral is_a Exposure` and a separate `Exposure secured_by Collateral` relation. Source finding context: Inheritance makes collateral structurally depend on exposure attributes, constraining later collateral-specific lifecycle extensions. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / entities.Collateral Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `Exposure` definition; `Collateral` definition with `is_a: Exposure`; relation `Exposure -> Collateral` with `kind: secured_by`. Source finding context: `Collateral` is simultaneously modeled as an `Exposure` subtype and as the asset secured by an `Exposure`, creating a type-level conflict for the same entity role. Source finding context: The conflict is formed by explicit claims in the artifact: `Exposure` is the credit exposure, `Collateral` is the securing asset, `Collateral is_a Exposure`, and the relation `Exposure secured_by Collateral` uses Collateral as the secured asset target. These cannot all be satisfied without collapsing exposure and collateral asset into the same class, which weakens the ontology as a shared concept authority for a risk engine and reporting system because systems may inherit exposure attributes onto collateral records or count collateral as exposure. Source finding context: Remove `is_a: Exposure` from `Collateral` and keep collateral connected through the `Exposure -> Collateral secured_by` relation; if a shared parent is needed, introduce a neutral parent such as `RiskObject` only with attributes valid for both concepts. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-001 Source finding context: Shared credit-risk concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: When consumers interpret `is_a` as inheritance/subtyping, collateral assets become valid Exposure instances while also serving as assets securing exposures. Source finding context: This can make type-based consumers apply exposure fields, aggregation, or reporting logic to collateral records, undermining the declared shared taxonomy contract. Source finding context: The ontology uses `is_a` to connect Collateral to Exposure even though Collateral's own definition and relation role describe a secured asset, not a credit exposure. Source finding context: Collateral is declared as an asset that secures an exposure and as `is_a: Exposure`. Source finding context: Exposure is separately defined as the credit exposure to a borrower, and the relation declares Exposure as secured by Collateral. Source finding context: Together these claims require Collateral to be both a kind of Exposure and the asset target that secures an Exposure. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml: Collateral entity Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:39-46,85-88 Source finding context: Collateral is modeled as a subtype of Exposure even though its definition makes it a securing asset, not a credit exposure. Source finding context: A collateral asset and a credit exposure are different ontological kinds: one is the pledged asset, the other is the credit position being secured. Treating collateral as an Exposure weakens the shared concept standard because a risk engine or report can classify collateral records as exposure records or apply exposure attributes/aggregation semantics to assets. Source finding context: Remove `Collateral.is_a: Exposure`; keep Collateral as a separate asset entity connected through `Exposure secured_by Collateral`. If a shared superclass is needed, introduce a neutral parent such as `RiskObject` only after defining common semantics explicitly. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-001 Source finding context: A shared concept authority for risk engines and reporting systems covering exposure and collateral concepts. Source finding context: Any consumer that interprets `is_a` semantically will treat collateral assets as credit exposures despite the target also defining them as securing assets. Source finding context: This can corrupt classification, aggregation, and reporting semantics at the boundary between exposure records and collateral records. Source finding context: The subtype assertion uses `Exposure` as the parent for `Collateral` although the target definition assigns Collateral the meaning of a securing asset. Source finding context: Collateral is defined as an asset securing an exposure and the base unit of collateral appraisal. Source finding context: The same entity is declared `is_a: Exposure`. Source finding context: The relation section separately models Exposure as `secured_by` Collateral, confirming the intended distinction between exposure and collateral asset.
  - failure condition: A consumer interprets is_a as inheritance/subtyping, or collateral coverage expands to pooled collateral, multiple valuations, lien priority, guarantees, substitutions, or other collateral-specific lifecycles. Source finding context: When collateral coverage expands beyond one asset directly attached to one exposure, the inherited exposure structure forces collateral records to carry exposure fields or requires a breaking remodel. Source finding context: When consumers interpret `is_a` as inheritance/subtyping, collateral assets become valid Exposure instances while also serving as assets securing exposures. Source finding context: Any consumer that interprets `is_a` semantically will treat collateral assets as credit exposures despite the target also defining them as securing assets.
  - candidates: fix_now
  - rationale: Remove `Collateral is_a Exposure` and model Collateral as a separate asset/support concept. Connect it to Exposure through a relationship entity such as `SecurityAgreement` or `CollateralLink` that can carry allocation, priority, valuation, and effective-date semantics, so collateral-specific lifecycle behavior can evolve without inheriting exposure attributes or breaking consumers.
  - remediation: Remove `Collateral is_a Exposure` and model Collateral as a separate asset/support concept. Connect it to Exposure through a relationship entity such as `SecurityAgreement` or `CollateralLink` that can carry allocation, priority, valuation, and effective-date semantics, so collateral-specific lifecycle behavior can evolve without inheriting exposure attributes or breaking consumers.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
- issue-002 (medium)
  - issue: 이 이슈는 차주 등급, 리스크 엔진 등급, 익스포저 등급의 병존 자체가 아니라, 그 사이의 변환 기준을 온톨로지의 canonical 개념으로 소유하지 않고 각 소비 시스템에 맡긴 점이 문제다. 따라서 공유 온톨로지는 등급 의미와 변환 기준의 단일 기준 역할을 하지 못한다.
  - target: 등급 스케일 변환을 각 소비 시스템에 맡겨 공유 개념 기준 목적을 훼손한다. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml rating scale notes and RiskRating.grade Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:20-22,53-56,97-98; .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-8 Source finding context: 이 온톨로지의 목적은 엔진과 보고 시스템이 공유할 개념 기준을 제공하는 것이다. 그런데 등급 변환의 의미와 책임을 각 소비 시스템으로 분산하면 같은 차주/익스포저 등급이 시스템별 변환표, 버전, 적용일에 따라 달라질 수 있다. 이는 역사적 병존이라는 국소 현실을 보존하는 대신 공유 기준이라는 상위 목적을 양보한 숨은 트레이드오프다. Source finding context: 세 등급 스케일을 인정하되, canonical RatingScale과 RatingMapping 개념을 온톨로지 안에 추가하라. source_scale, target_scale, source_grade, target_grade, mapping_version, owner, effective_from/effective_to, precedence를 정의하고 외부 위키는 비권위 참고가 아니라 해당 규칙의 출처/운영 절차로만 연결하라. Source finding context: 엔진, 보고서, 기타 소비 시스템이 각자 다른 변환표나 다른 시점의 위키 규칙으로 internal_rating/RiskRating/Exposure risk_grade를 변환하는 경우. Source finding context: 공유 온톨로지가 등급 의미의 단일 기준이 되지 못하고, 시스템별 해석 차이를 정당화하는 문서가 되어 보고 및 리스크 판단 간 불일치를 만든다. Source finding context: 등급 스케일 병존을 canonical mapping concept으로 승격하지 않고 소비 시스템별 변환 책임으로 남겼다. Source finding context: 차주 등급, 엔진 등급, 익스포저 등급의 세 스케일이 병존한다. Source finding context: 변환은 각 소비 시스템이 수행한다고 명시되어 있다. Source finding context: 온톨로지는 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 공유하는 개념 권위 문서를 지향한다.
  - failure condition: 엔진, 보고서, 기타 소비 시스템이 각자 다른 변환표나 다른 시점의 위키 규칙으로 internal_rating/RiskRating/Exposure risk_grade를 변환하는 경우.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: 세 등급 스케일은 인정하되, 온톨로지 안에 canonical RatingScale과 RatingMapping을 추가해야 한다. RatingMapping에는 source_scale, target_scale, source_grade, target_grade, mapping_version, owner, effective_from/effective_to, precedence를 포함해 변환 권위와 적용 순서를 명시해야 한다. 외부 위키는 권위 있는 변환 기준 자체가 아니라 해당 규칙의 출처나 운영 절차로 연결해야 하며, 이 작업은 소비 시스템 구현보다 먼저 닫혀야 한다.
  - remediation: 세 등급 스케일은 인정하되, 온톨로지 안에 canonical RatingScale과 RatingMapping을 추가해야 한다. RatingMapping에는 source_scale, target_scale, source_grade, target_grade, mapping_version, owner, effective_from/effective_to, precedence를 포함해 변환 권위와 적용 순서를 명시해야 한다. 외부 위키는 권위 있는 변환 기준 자체가 아니라 해당 규칙의 출처나 운영 절차로 연결해야 하며, 이 작업은 소비 시스템 구현보다 먼저 닫혀야 한다.

- issue-003 (medium)
  - issue: 한도 소진율과 RiskAppetite 준수 상태는 현재 공유된 시간 기준과 FX 권위 없이 월말 배치 ExposureAggregate 및 시스템별 환율표에 의존하므로, 같은 포트폴리오와 같은 판단 시점에서도 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 서로 다른 utilization 또는 breached/compliant 상태를 산출할 수 있다.
  - target: 한도 소진율과 RiskAppetite 준수 판단이 시스템별 환율표와 월말 배치 집계에 의존해 시간성·권위 목적에서 drift 된다. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml Limit utilization and ExposureAggregate temporality Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:64-76,78-83,92-95; .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/review-value-alignment-criteria.yaml:5-8 Source finding context: RiskAppetite 준수 여부는 운영상 민감한 개념인데, 입력 총액은 월말 기준으로 낡을 수 있고 환산 기준은 시스템별로 달라질 수 있다. 공유 개념 권위 문서의 목적상 같은 시점의 같은 포트폴리오에 대해 엔진과 보고 시스템이 같은 준수 상태를 재현해야 하지만, 현재 설계는 시간 기준과 환율 권위를 분산시켜 서로 다른 소진율과 위반 여부를 허용한다. Source finding context: Limit 소진율과 RiskAppetite 준수 판단에 canonical valuation_time/as_of, aggregation_cutoff, freshness_status, fx_rate_source, fx_rate_timestamp, currency_basis를 추가하라. 월말 배치 값은 stale 가능성을 명시하고, 일중 판단이 필요한 준수 상태에는 별도 current exposure source 또는 'not current' 상태를 모델링하라. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/axiology.findings.yaml#axiology-candidate-003 Source finding context: 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 한도 소진율 및 RiskAppetite 준수 상태를 동일한 권위와 시간 기준으로 공유하는 것. Source finding context: 일중 신규 여신이 존재하거나 통화가 다른 한도/익스포저를 다룰 때, 시스템별 환율표와 월말 집계 시점 차이로 소진율 또는 breached/compliant 상태가 달라지는 경우. Source finding context: 위반 상태라는 핵심 운영 신호가 공유 온톨로지에서 재현 가능한 권위 값이 아니게 되어 보고 지연, 한도 초과 미탐지, 엔진-보고 불일치 위험을 만든다. Source finding context: 한도/준수 판단에 필요한 시간 기준과 환율 권위를 공유 개념으로 닫지 않고 소비 시스템과 배치 주기에 맡겼다. Source finding context: ExposureAggregate.total_amount는 월말 배치 저장 값이며 일중 신규 여신은 다음 배치까지 미반영된다. Source finding context: 통화가 다르면 각 시스템의 당일 환율 테이블로 Limit 소진율을 계산한다.   - failure condition: 일중 신규 여신이 존재하거나 통화가 다른 한도/익스포저를 다룰 때, 시스템별 환율표와 월말 집계 시점 차이로 소진율 또는 breached/compliant 상태가 달라지는 경우.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Limit 소진율과 RiskAppetite 준수 판단에 canonical valuation_time/as_of, aggregation_cutoff, freshness_status, fx_rate_source, fx_rate_timestamp, currency_basis를 모델링해야 한다. 월말 배치 값은 stale 가능성을 명시하고, 일중 준수 판단이 필요한 경우 별도 current exposure source 또는 'not current' 상태를 두어 현재성 없는 값을 현재 준수 상태처럼 소비하지 못하게 해야 한다.

- issue-004 (medium)
  - issue: Default and delinquency states are materially missing from the shared credit-risk ontology as a minimal external-authority lifecycle-status reference, so the ontology cannot provide a common basis for post-transition exposure classification across risk engines and reporting.
  - target: The ontology leaves delinquency/default lifecycle states outside the shared credit-risk concept standard even though those states affect risk classification, reporting, limits, and collateral treatment. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / Exposure and notes[1] Source finding context: Embedded materialized input: Exposure entity; notes[1] Source finding context: 연체·부실 상태를 범위 밖으로 제외해 여신 리스크 분류의 상태 lifecycle이 정상 익스포저 중심으로만 닫히고, 부실 전이 이후의 리스크 상태 범주가 누락된다. Source finding context: 대상은 여신 리스크 관리의 개념 모델로서 익스포저, 등급, 한도, 담보를 정의하고 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 공유할 기준을 지향한다. 그러나 여신 리스크에서 익스포저 lifecycle의 핵심 상태인 연체·부실을 제외하면, 리스크 엔진 등급과 보고 시스템의 상태 기반 집계가 같은 차주/익스포저를 어느 단계로 분류해야 하는지 공유할 수 없다. 별도 시스템이 소유하더라도 최소한 외부 권위 상태 참조, 기준시점, 수신 이벤트 개념은 필요하다. Source finding context: DefaultStatus 또는 ExposureLifecycleStatus를 온톨로지에 최소 참조 개념으로 포함하고, source_system, status, status_effective_at, status_received_at, status_history_ref를 둔다. 연체관리 시스템이 권위 원본이라면 out-of-scope 배제가 아니라 외부 권위 참조로 명시해 보고와 엔진이 동일 상태를 사용할 수 있게 한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-004 Source finding context: 여신 리스크 분류 온톨로지의 주요 리스크 상태 포괄성과 리스크 엔진·보고 시스템 간 상태 기준 공유 Source finding context: 익스포저가 연체 또는 부실로 전이되어 등급, 한도 소진, 담보 평가, 보고 집계가 상태에 따라 달라져야 하는 경우 Source finding context: 온톨로지가 정상/등급/한도 중심 상태만 제공하고 부실 상태 참조를 배제하므로 주요 리스크 lifecycle 구간이 공유 기준 밖에 남아 보고·엔진 간 분류 불일치가 발생할 수 있다. This should preserve the external system as the authority while giving risk-engine and reporting consumers the same lifecycle status basis; richer status vocabulary and transition semantics can be defined later once authoritative evidence is available.
  - remediation: Add a minimal DefaultStatus or ExposureLifecycleStatus reference concept linked to the external authority, including source_system, status, status_effective_at, status_received_at, and status_history_ref. This should preserve the external system as the authority while giving risk-engine and reporting consumers the same lifecycle status basis; richer status vocabulary and transition semantics can be defined later once authoritative evidence is available.

- issue-006 (medium)
  - issue: Issue-006 is a material utilization-calculation provenance and authority gap: limit utilization is defined from stored exposure aggregates, approved limits, and FX conversion, but the ontology lacks canonical snapshot, version, source, cutoff/as-of, and valuation metadata needed to replay the calculation consistently.
  - target: Shared-root issue across finding-009, finding-006: The ontology combines stored exposure aggregates, approved limits, and FX conversion without canonical snapshot/version/source metadata for replayable utilization calculations. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / ExposureAggregate and classification_rules[1] Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `ExposureAggregate.total_amount` note says `월말 배치로 계산해 저장. 일중 신규 여신은 다음 배치까지 미반영.`; classification rule says `Limit 소진율 = ExposureAggregate.total_amount / Limit.approved_amount. 통화가 다른 경우 환산은 각 시스템의 당일 환율 테이블을 사용한다.` Source finding context: Exposure aggregation and limit-utilization rules are not temporally versioned, so adding intraday products, currencies, or reporting cutoffs can break continuity of limit calculations. Source finding context: A credit risk taxonomy that must serve both engines and reports needs stable temporal semantics for replay, audit, and future product/currency expansion. Here, intraday exposures are explicitly excluded until the next batch, while limit utilization uses that stale aggregate and system-local FX. When new intraday facilities, currencies, or reporting frequencies are added, the existing concepts cannot absorb them without changing calculation semantics or accepting inconsistent utilization outputs. Source finding context: Introduce explicit temporal and valuation concepts: `ExposureSnapshot` or `ExposureAggregate` with calculation_frequency, included_exposure_cutoff, as_of timestamp, source batch/run id, and FX source/rate_time. Define limit utilization against a named snapshot and canonical FX policy. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-002 Source finding context: Shared risk-engine and reporting basis for limit utilization and exposure aggregation. Source finding context: When the ontology must support intraday lending, additional currencies, or different report cutoffs, the same borrower and limit can receive different utilization results depending on batch timing and each system's FX table. Source finding context: The ontology is not evolution-resilient for temporal expansion: future reporting or engine changes require reworking core aggregate semantics and may disrupt comparability with historical stored totals. Source finding context: The ontology combines stored batch aggregates with system-local FX conversion but lacks canonical snapshot/version and valuation-time concepts. Source finding context: `ExposureAggregate.total_amount` is defined as the total of all exposure amounts but is stored from a month-end batch that omits intraday new credit until the next batch. Source finding context: Limit utilization is computed from that aggregate and approved limit amount. Source finding context: Currency conversion is delegated to each system's same-day FX table, leaving no stable valuation authority in the ontology. Source finding context: Future intraday, multi-currency, or higher-frequency reporting changes can alter utilization results without a versioned snapshot or FX policy preserving continuity. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / Limit.approved_amount and classification_rules[1] Source finding context: Embedded materialized input: Limit.attributes.approved_amount note; classification_rules[1] Source finding context: 한도와 환율처럼 복수 시스템에 걸쳐 관리되는 값에 단일 원본 권위와 기준시점 개념이 누락되어 한도 소진율 계산 기준이 분산된다. Source finding context: Limit 소진율은 ExposureAggregate.total_amount / Limit.approved_amount로 정의되어 리스크 위반 판단과 보고에 직접 쓰일 수 있는 지표다. 그런데 구성 값의 단일 권위와 환산 기준이 없으면 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 같은 차주·같은 날짜에 서로 다른 소진율을 계산할 수 있으며, 공유 개념 기준으로서의 역할이 약해진다. Source finding context: LimitSourceAuthority 또는 BookOfRecord 개념을 추가해 승인 시스템, CRM, 환율 테이블의 역할과 우선순위를 명시한다. CurrencyConversionRate에는 rate_source, rate_timestamp, effective_date, currency_pair, version을 두고, LimitUtilization 계산에는 사용한 원천값과 환율 기준을 참조하게 한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-003 Source finding context: 리스크 엔진과 보고 시스템이 공유하는 한도 소진율 및 통화 환산 기준 Source finding context: 승인 시스템과 CRM의 한도 값이 다르거나 각 시스템의 당일 환율 테이블이 다른 값을 제공하는 경우 Source finding context: 소진율 계산의 입력 권위와 환산 기준이 분산되어 동일 익스포저에 대해 시스템별 결과가 달라질 수 있고, RiskAppetite 위반 판단 또는 보고 숫자의 재현성이 떨어진다. Source finding context: 복수 시스템 관리 값과 환산 기준에 대한 권위 원본 및 우선순위 개념이 없는 범위 결손 Source finding context: Limit.approved_amount는 승인 시스템 값이지만 CRM의 available_limit과 합산 관리된다고 설명된다. Source finding context: 통화 환산은 각 시스템의 당일 환율 테이블을 사용한다고 되어 있어 환산 입력도 시스템별로 분산된다. Source finding context: 온톨로지에는 source of record, 우선순위, 환율 기준시각/버전 개념이 없다. Source finding context: 따라서 Limit 소진율을 공유 계산 기준으로 삼는 데 필요한 권위 범위가 닫혀 있지 않다.
  - failure condition: When the ontology must support intraday lending, additional currencies, or different report cutoffs, the same borrower and limit can receive different utilization results depending on batch timing and each system's FX table. / 승인 시스템과 CRM의 한도 값이 다르거나 각 시스템의 당일 환율 테이블이 다른 값을 제공하는 경우
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Fix this before release by making utilization depend on explicit temporal and valuation concepts: define ExposureSnapshot or extend ExposureAggregate with calculation_frequency, included_exposure_cutoff, as_of timestamp, source batch/run id, and version; add source-of-record or BookOfRecord/LimitSourceAuthority concepts for limit values; add CurrencyConversionRate metadata such as rate_source, rate_timestamp, effective_date, currency_pair, and version. LimitUtilization should reference the named snapshot, authoritative limit source, and canonical FX policy so replay and reporting use the same inputs.
  - remediation: Fix this before release by making utilization depend on explicit temporal and valuation concepts: define ExposureSnapshot or extend ExposureAggregate with calculation_frequency, included_exposure_cutoff, as_of timestamp, source batch/run id, and version; add source-of-record or BookOfRecord/LimitSourceAuthority concepts for limit values; add CurrencyConversionRate metadata such as rate_source, rate_timestamp, effective_date, currency_pair, and version. LimitUtilization should reference the named snapshot, authoritative limit source, and canonical FX policy so replay and reporting use the same inputs.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
- issue-007 (medium)
  - issue: Issue-007 remains a material evolvability problem: core credit-risk classifications are modeled as closed inline enums, so ordinary growth in segments, products, collateral classes, or grades requires schema and validator edits instead of additive taxonomy management.
  - target: The ontology makes core categories such as segment, product type, collateral type, and grades brittle under normal portfolio, regulatory, or reporting evolution. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml / enumerated category attributes Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `Borrower.segment` values `[retail, sme, corporate]`; `Exposure.product_type` values `[term_loan, revolving, guarantee]`; `Collateral.collateral_type` values `[real_estate, deposit, securities]`; `Exposure.risk_grade` and `RiskRating.grade` are also closed enum lists. Source finding context: Core business classifications are modeled as closed inline enums, so new borrower segments, products, collateral classes, or grades require schema edits rather than additive extension. Source finding context: For an evolving credit-risk concept standard, categories such as product type, collateral type, segment, and grade are expected to expand as portfolios, regulations, and reporting needs change. Inline closed enums force changes directly into existing attribute definitions, increasing migration pressure and making it harder to preserve historical category meaning or support consumer compatibility. Source finding context: Promote volatile classifications to managed taxonomy concepts such as `ProductType`, `CollateralType`, `BorrowerSegment`, and `GradeCode` with stable ids, labels, active/deprecated status, version/effective dates, and parent/category relations. Keep attributes as references to those taxonomy entries. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-003 Source finding context: Extensible shared classification ontology for credit-risk engine and reporting concepts. Source finding context: When a new segment, product, collateral class, or grade is introduced, the ontology must edit existing enum declarations and downstream validators instead of adding a new taxonomy record with compatibility metadata. Source finding context: This creates brittle evolution and migration risk in the core classification surface, especially for systems that rely on the ontology as a shared validation and reporting basis. Source finding context: The ontology encodes volatile classification vocabularies as closed attribute-local enums instead of first-class extensible taxonomy concepts. Source finding context: Multiple core classification axes are closed inline enum lists inside attributes. Source finding context: Inline enum expansion requires modifying existing attribute schemas rather than adding separate taxonomy records. Source finding context: Without lifecycle or effective-version metadata, future category changes cannot preserve historical meaning or compatibility in the ontology itself.
  - failure condition: A new borrower segment, product type, collateral class, or grade must be introduced while preserving historical meaning and downstream compatibility. Source finding context: When a new segment, product, collateral class, or grade is introduced, the ontology must edit existing enum declarations and downstream validators instead of adding a new taxonomy record with compatibility metadata.
  - candidates: follow_up
  - rationale: Promote these volatile classifications to managed taxonomy concepts such as ProductType, CollateralType, BorrowerSegment, and GradeCode, with stable ids, labels, aliases, status, effective/version dates, and parent/category relations. Then keep the entity attributes as references to those taxonomy entries so category growth is additive and downstream compatibility can be managed explicitly.
  - remediation: Promote these volatile classifications to managed taxonomy concepts such as ProductType, CollateralType, BorrowerSegment, and GradeCode, with stable ids, labels, aliases, status, effective/version dates, and parent/category relations. Then keep the entity attributes as references to those taxonomy entries so category growth is additive and downstream compatibility can be managed explicitly.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
- issue-009 (medium)
  - issue: Issue-009 is material: Exposure.ltv is presented as a collateralized risk ratio, but the ontology does not define whether that value is calculated, manually entered, snapshotted, or overridden. That leaves consumers unable to know which LTV value is authoritative for risk rules or reporting.
  - target: The ontology lets Exposure.ltv drift from Exposure.amount and Collateral.appraised_value without saying whether calculated, entered, or overridden LTV is authoritative. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml: Exposure.ltv Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:24-37,39-46 Source finding context: `ltv` is named as a collateral ratio but modeled as an independent underwriter input while its source concepts coexist in the ontology. Source finding context: A ratio field stored as an independent input can diverge from the amount and appraisal values that define the risk meaning of the ratio. For a shared ontology, this makes it unclear whether LTV is authoritative input, derived value, or historical snapshot. Source finding context: Define `ltv` as a derived or snapshot attribute with explicit formula, denominator source, timestamp, and authority. If manual override is allowed, split it from calculated LTV, for example `calculated_ltv` and `approved_ltv_override`, with override authority and reason. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002 Source finding context: Shared semantic basis for risk-engine and reporting use of collateralized exposure risk measures. Source finding context: When Exposure.amount or Collateral.appraised_value changes, the independently entered `ltv` can retain a different value without the ontology saying which value is authoritative. Source finding context: Risk rules and reports can use inconsistent LTV semantics for the same exposure, reducing trust in classification and limit monitoring outputs. Source finding context: The ontology names `ltv` as a ratio-like risk measure but gives it input-field semantics instead of derived/snapshot semantics. Source finding context: Exposure contains `amount`, and Collateral contains `appraised_value` and `appraised_at`. Source finding context: Exposure also contains `ltv` with the note that it is entered by an underwriter at review time. Source finding context: Because the target does not define formula, timestamp, or override semantics for `ltv`, the ratio can drift from its source concepts.
  - failure condition: Exposure.amount or Collateral.appraised_value changes while the independently entered ltv value remains different, or a consumer needs to decide whether to trust calculated or entered LTV. Source finding context: When Exposure.amount or Collateral.appraised_value changes, the independently entered `ltv` can retain a different value without the ontology saying which value is authoritative.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Define LTV as a governed derived or snapshot measure with explicit formula, denominator and valuation source, timestamp, and authority. If manual override is allowed, split calculated LTV from the approved override and include override reason, approval authority, and related metadata. Preserve the dependency on the broader exposure-collateral valuation/link lifecycle, but do not let that broader dependency postpone fixing LTV authority semantics in the target model.
  - remediation: Define LTV as a governed derived or snapshot measure with explicit formula, denominator and valuation source, timestamp, and authority. If manual override is allowed, split calculated LTV from the approved override and include override reason, approval authority, and related metadata. Preserve the dependency on the broader exposure-collateral valuation/link lifecycle, but do not let that broader dependency postpone fixing LTV authority semantics in the target model.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
- issue-010 (medium)
  - issue: The ontology materially fails to distinguish three non-equivalent credit grade concepts: borrower internal rating, engine-produced borrower rating, and exposure reporting grade. Because these are presented as loosely related credit/risk grades without canonical mapping or authority, consumers can treat incompatible scales as if they were interchangeable.
  - target: Three non-equivalent rating scales are all presented as credit/risk grades without a canonical semantic mapping or distinct concept names. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml: credit rating concepts Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:20-22,31-34,48-57,92-99 Source finding context: These are not merely alternate labels; they encode different authorities, units, and possibly different subjects: borrower-level internal rating, engine-produced borrower rating, and exposure-level reporting grade. Leaving them as loosely related grades makes same-looking 'rating/grade' semantics depend on the consuming system. Source finding context: Name the concepts by subject and authority, such as `borrower_internal_rating`, `engine_borrower_rating`, and `exposure_reporting_grade`; add an explicit canonical mapping/derivation artifact with owner, effective time, and override semantics for report-side adjustments. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-003 Source finding context: A concept authority shared by the risk engine and reporting system for credit-grade classification. Source finding context: A consumer must interpret or convert rating scales locally because the ontology does not establish one canonical semantic mapping or distinguish adjusted report grades from engine grades. Source finding context: Risk engine outputs and reporting classifications can disagree while both appear valid under the ontology, weakening the ontology as the shared concept standard. Source finding context: The ontology preserves historically separate rating scales without assigning a canonical semantic authority or explicit mapping inside the shared concept artifact. Source finding context: Borrower, RiskRating, and Exposure each define a grade/rating field with distinct scales and subjects. Source finding context: The classification rule says Exposure.risk_grade derives from RiskRating.grade but can be manually adjusted by the reporting team. Source finding context: The notes say the three scales coexist for historical reasons and conversion is performed by each consuming system.
  - failure condition: A consumer must interpret or convert rating scales locally because the ontology does not establish one canonical semantic mapping or distinguish adjusted report grades from engine grades.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Fix the issue by naming the concepts according to subject and authority, for example borrower_internal_rating, engine_borrower_rating, and exposure_reporting_grade, and by adding a canonical mapping or derivation artifact. That artifact should define the mapping owner, effective time/versioning, and override semantics for reporting adjustments so engine output, borrower-level rating, and report-grade classification have explicit relationships rather than implicit consumer-specific conversions.
  - remediation: Fix the issue by naming the concepts according to subject and authority, for example borrower_internal_rating, engine_borrower_rating, and exposure_reporting_grade, and by adding a canonical mapping or derivation artifact. That artifact should define the mapping owner, effective time/versioning, and override semantics for reporting adjustments so engine output, borrower-level rating, and report-grade classification have explicit relationships rather than implicit consumer-specific conversions.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
- issue-011 (medium)
  - issue: RiskAppetite compliance is materially under-modeled: RiskAppetite should represent the policy threshold, while compliance should be a separate scoped evaluation result over an ExposureAggregate at a specific time.
  - target: The ontology cannot reliably compute, audit, or traverse RiskAppetite compliance because the threshold, evaluation result, subject aggregate, and time basis are not separated and structurally connected. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml: RiskAppetite entity Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:69-83,92-94 Source finding context: RiskAppetite conflates a policy threshold with a compliance result state. Source finding context: A threshold policy and a breach/compliance observation have different semantics and lifecycles. Placing the status on RiskAppetite makes the appetite object appear to change from compliant to breached, instead of producing a status for a specific aggregate evaluated against the appetite. Source finding context: Keep RiskAppetite as the policy/threshold concept and introduce a separate evaluation concept such as `RiskAppetiteCompliance` with references to RiskAppetite and ExposureAggregate plus `as_of` and `status`. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-004 Source finding context: Shared concept authority for limit/appetite compliance across engine and reporting outputs. Source finding context: A consumer treats `RiskAppetite.compliance_status` as intrinsic to the appetite threshold rather than as an evaluation result for a specific exposure aggregate and time. Source finding context: Reports or engine checks can lose the subject and time basis of a breach, making compliance status ambiguous and hard to reconcile. Source finding context: The entity combines the meaning of risk appetite policy with the meaning of compliance evaluation result. Source finding context: ExposureAggregate is a borrower-level total exposure with an `as_of` date. Source finding context: RiskAppetite is defined as a threshold and also carries `compliance_status`. Source finding context: The definition states breach status is determined when ExposureAggregate exceeds RiskAppetite, making status an evaluation result rather than an intrinsic property of the appetite threshold. Source finding context: credit-risk-ontology.yaml entities.RiskAppetite / relations Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:78 and .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:85 Source finding context: RiskAppetite is structurally orphaned despite being defined as the compliance target for ExposureAggregate. Source finding context: The declared target is a shared concept authority for a risk engine and reporting system. With no structural connection, consumers cannot traverse from borrower exposure totals to the appetite threshold or compliance status using the ontology itself. The evidence supports this because RiskAppetite is declared at lines 78-83, while the complete relations list at lines 85-90 connects Borrower, Exposure, Collateral, RiskRating, Limit, and ExposureAggregate but omits RiskAppetite entirely. Source finding context: Add explicit structural relations and/or ref attributes that connect RiskAppetite to the entity it constrains, for example ExposureAggregate -> RiskAppetite with a kind such as tested_against or governed_by, plus the required scope keys such as borrower segment, product type, currency, and as_of if those determine which threshold applies. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-acee24d9/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-001 Source finding context: The ontology's stated purpose as a shared concept authority for risk engine and reporting systems. Source finding context: When a consumer needs to compute, audit, or report RiskAppetite compliance from ExposureAggregate values, the target threshold/status concept is not reachable from the exposure aggregate graph. Source finding context: This weakens review trust because a central compliance concept is present but disconnected; different consumers may invent separate joins or scoping assumptions, producing inconsistent compliance outputs from the same ontology. Source finding context: RiskAppetite was added as an entity and rule target without adding the required graph edge or reference that binds it to ExposureAggregate or another scoping authority. Source finding context: RiskAppetite is declared as an entity with threshold and compliance_status attributes. Source finding context: Its definition depends on a comparison between ExposureAggregate and RiskAppetite. Source finding context: The ontology's relations section omits any relation involving RiskAppetite, leaving that comparison outside the navigable structure.
  - failure condition: A consumer needs to compute, audit, or report compliance from ExposureAggregate values, or treats RiskAppetite.compliance_status as intrinsic to the threshold rather than an evaluation result for a specific subject and time. Source finding context: A consumer treats `RiskAppetite.compliance_status` as intrinsic to the appetite threshold rather than as an evaluation result for a specific exposure aggregate and time. Source finding context: When a consumer needs to compute, audit, or report RiskAppetite compliance from ExposureAggregate values, the target threshold/status concept is not reachable from the exposure aggregate graph.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Keep RiskAppetite as the policy/threshold concept and add a RiskAppetiteCompliance evaluation concept or explicit relation that binds RiskAppetite, ExposureAggregate, applicable scope keys, as_of/evaluation time, and status. This should be fixed in the target before release because downstream engine and reporting consumers need one authoritative structure for deriving and auditing compliance rather than parallel consumer-specific interpretations.
  - remediation: Keep RiskAppetite as the policy/threshold concept and add a RiskAppetiteCompliance evaluation concept or explicit relation that binds RiskAppetite, ExposureAggregate, applicable scope keys, as_of/evaluation time, and status. This should be fixed in the target before release because downstream engine and reporting consumers need one authoritative structure for deriving and auditing compliance rather than parallel consumer-specific interpretations.
